后疫情时代交织的传染病威胁:监测脆弱性、大流行风险和全球准备度

Converging infectious disease threats in the post-COVID era: surveillance fragility, pandemic risk, and global preparedness
作者： Abdi Y, Issack Z
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在应对后疫情时代传染病威胁日益交织的问题,特别是关注监测系统的脆弱性以及未来大流行的潜在风险。背景强调了人兽共患病、抗菌药物耐药性和气候敏感性疾病发病率的上升,作为全球健康面临的重大挑战。
设计与对象
本研究对2020年1月至2026年6月期间发表的文献进行了叙述性综述,并有意纳入2010年至2019年的基础研究,以提供流行病学和生态学背景。它分析了全球大流行和小流行疾病暴发的趋势,包括与禽流感相关的疾病。
核心方法
本研究采用系统性文献检索策略,在PubMed、Scopus和Web of Science上进行搜索,并补充了来自世界卫生组织、非洲疾病预防控制中心和世界银行的灰色文献。还进行了手动反向引用检索以识别更多相关来源,并排除了没有公共卫生或监测背景的研究。
主要结果
Between September and November 2025, 19 human HPAI infections were reported across four countries, including two deaths. As of September 2024, more than 29,000 cases of MDR-TB or rifampicin-resistant TB had been reported, with over 800 fatalities. In 2024, 14.1 million dengue cases were reported globally, a twofold increase from 2023 and a 12-fold rise compared to 2014.
解释与创新
作者认为,猴痘的再次出现以及MDR-TB和登革热病例的增加凸显了传染病威胁的持续性和演变性。他们强调需要对这些交织的风险进行整合分析,以指导全球准备度。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现强调了加强全球监测系统和应对抗菌药物耐药性的紧迫性,特别是在生物多样性高但基础设施有限的地区。它们还突出了监测人兽共患病和气候敏感性病原体的重要性,以减轻未来大流行的风险。
局限与证据强度
本研究的局限性在于其依赖叙述性综述,且在选择来源时可能存在偏倚。证据强度中等,因为其综合了现有数据而非提出新的实证发现,且结果的普遍性可能受到特定地区和病原体的关注限制。

Converging infectious disease threats in the post-COVID era: surveillance fragility, pandemic risk, and global preparedness

Authors: Abdi Y, Issack Z
Original abstract (English)
This narrative review synthesizes contemporary evidence on three converging drivers of pandemic risk zoonotic emergence, antimicrobial resistance, and climate-sensitive disease expansion and examines how structural failures in surveillance and global health governance amplify their impact. Drawing on recent epidemiological analyses, policy reports, and One Health evaluations, the review maps post-COVID surges in zoonotic outbreaks, drug-resistant infections, and vector and water-borne diseases, and links these trends to fragmented laboratory networks, inequitable genomic capacity, and weak integration across human, animal, and environmental systems. The review argues that without redistributive investment in One Health integration, regional genomic platforms, community-based surveillance, and enforceable IHR-based accountability, the world will continue to detect converging threats late and respond inequitably, leaving pandemic preparedness structurally fragile in the post-COVID era.Keywords: antimicrobial resistance, climate-sensitive diseases, infectious disease surveillance, one health, pandemic preparedness 2.MethodologyTo construct this integrative narrative review, a targeted literature search was conducted to identify peer-reviewed articles, epidemiological reports, and global health policy documents. AbstractThe COVID-19 pandemic exposed profound weaknesses in global infectious disease surveillance yet, in its aftermath, pandemic-prone threats have intensified rather than receded. It further interrogates the geopolitical and socioeconomic determinants of vulnerability, including conflict-related surveillance collapse, vaccine inequity, and the political economy of pandemic financing. Emerging digital tools from AI-enabled forecasting to wastewater and social me...
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the growing convergence of infectious disease threats in the post-COVID era, particularly focusing on the fragility of surveillance systems and the risk of future pandemics. The background highlights the increasing prevalence of zoonotic diseases, antimicrobial resistance, and climate-sensitive diseases as critical global health challenges.
Design and population
The study conducted a narrative review of literature published between January 2020 and June 2026, with intentional inclusion of foundational studies from 2010–2019 to provide epidemiological and ecological context. It analyzed global trends in pandemic-prone and epidemic-prone disease outbreaks, including those related to avian influenza.
Core methods
The study employed a systematic search strategy across PubMed, Scopus, and Web of Science, supplemented by grey literature from WHO, Africa CDC, and the World Bank. A manual backward citation search was also performed to identify additional relevant sources, and studies without a public health or surveillance context were excluded.
Main results
Between September and November 2025, 19 human HPAI infections were reported across four countries, including two deaths. As of September 2024, more than 29,000 cases of MDR-TB or rifampicin-resistant TB had been reported, with over 800 fatalities. In 2024, 14.1 million dengue cases were reported globally, a twofold increase from 2023 and a 12-fold rise compared to 2014.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret that the re-emergence of monkeypox and the increasing prevalence of MDR-TB and dengue cases underscore the persistent and evolving nature of infectious disease threats. They highlight the need for integrated analysis of these converging risks to inform global preparedness.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform the urgency of strengthening global surveillance systems and addressing antimicrobial resistance, particularly in regions with high biodiversity and limited infrastructure. They also emphasize the importance of monitoring zoonotic diseases and climate-sensitive pathogens to mitigate future pandemic risks.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on narrative reviews and the potential for bias in selecting sources. The evidence is moderate, as it synthesizes existing data rather than presenting new empirical findings, and the generalizability of results may be constrained by the focus on specific regions and pathogens.

H1N8病原体

H1N8 Pathogens
作者： Gustavo O. Fenner
摘要中文翻译南美迁徙涉禽中重组A/H1N8 禽流感病毒的基因组特征 本开放科学框架(OSF)项目提供了与论文《南美迁徙涉禽中重组A/H1N8 禽流感病毒的基因组特征》相关的补充计算数据和分析结果。该研究描述了一种从巴西南部帕拉库国家公园(PNLP)采集的白腹沙鹬(Calidris fuscicollis)中分离出的重组低致病性禽流感病毒(LPAI)亚型H1N8的基因组特征及其进化历史。基因组分析显示,该病毒由不同南美和北美禽流感谱系的基因片段组成,呈现出一种镶嵌结构。该OSF仓库补充了研究在GitHub上的计算仓库,并提供了支持论文中报告的贝叶斯系统发育、流行病动力学、流行地理学、宿主状态和重组分析的大规模分析档案。仓库内容 保存的文件包括:FASTq_222.zip — 与分离株PNLP_222相关的原始测序数据。jackknife_history_trees.zip — 来自jackknife敏感性分析的后验树历史记录。jackknife_logs.zip — jackknife分析产生的BEAST MCMC日志文件。NS_NA_logs_trees.zip — 与NS和NA分析相关的贝叶斯后验日志和树。Relaxed clock.zip — 在非相关对数正态放松分子钟框架下生成的贝叶斯分析结果。Strict clock.zip — 在严格分子钟框架下生成的贝叶斯分析结果。shared_backbone_logs.zip — 与共享内部基因骨架分析相关的贝叶斯分析结果。这些文件补充了GitHub仓库中提供的XML配置文件、最大似然树、最大支群可信度树、TreeSort分析和桑格测序图谱。GitHub仓库包含研究过程中使用的主要分析输入和组织好的补充文件。计算可重复性 完整的计算流程和分析文件已组织在关联的GitHub仓库中:PNLP_H1N8_Pathogens — GitHub仓库 该GitHub仓库包括放松和严格分子钟分析的BEAST XML文件、jackknife分析、共享骨架分析、TreeSort输入/输出文件、最大似然树、最大支群可信度树以及桑格测序图谱。此外,大规模的BEAST MCMC日志和后验树档案也存放在该OSF项目中,以方便访问研究的完整计算输出。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在描述从巴西南部帕拉库国家公园(PNLP)采集的白腹沙鹬中分离出的重组低致病性禽流感病毒(LPAI)亚型H1N8的基因组特征及其进化历史。由于禽流感病毒的日益关注及其可能重组并进化为更具致病性的形式,这项研究具有重要意义。
设计与对象
本研究涉及对从巴西南部帕拉库国家公园(PNLP)采集的白腹沙鹬中分离出的禽流感病毒的基因组分析。研究重点在于该病毒的进化历史和重组模式。
核心方法
方法包括使用计算工具进行基因组特征分析,存档文件包含原始测序数据、后验树历史记录、BEAST MCMC日志文件以及针对放松和严格分子钟分析、jackknife分析和共享骨架分析等不同框架的贝叶斯分析结果。
主要结果
基因组分析显示该病毒由不同南美和北美禽流感谱系的基因片段组成,呈现出一种镶嵌结构。
解释与创新
研究结果突显了禽流感病毒的遗传多样性和重组潜力,这可能对它们的进化和潜在的人兽共患病传播产生影响。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果有助于理解禽流感病毒的遗传动态,这对于监测、诊断和预防策略至关重要,以降低人兽共患病溢出和新病毒株出现的风险。
局限与证据强度
本研究仅限于计算和基因组分析,未报告直接的临床或流行病学结果。由于其依赖补充数据和计算方法,而缺乏详细的实验验证,证据强度中等。

H1N8 Pathogens

Authors: Gustavo O. Fenner
Original abstract (English)
Genomic Characterization of a Reassortant A/H1N8 Avian Influenza Virus in South American Migratory Shorebirds This Open Science Framework (OSF) project provides the supplementary computational data and analysis outputs associated with the manuscript “Genomic Characterization of a Reassortant A/H1N8 Avian Influenza Virus in South American Migratory Shorebirds.” The study describes the genomic characterization and evolutionary history of a reassortant low-pathogenic avian influenza virus (LPAI) subtype H1N8, isolated from a White-rumped Sandpiper (Calidris fuscicollis) sampled at Parque Nacional da Lagoa do Peixe (PNLP), southern Brazil. The genomic analyses identified a mosaic genome composed of gene segments associated with different South American and North American avian influenza lineages. This OSF repository complements the study's computational repository on GitHub and provides large analysis archives that support the Bayesian phylogenetic, phylodynamic, phylogeographic, host-state and reassortment analyses reported in the manuscript. Repository contents The archived files include: FASTq_222.zip — raw sequencing data associated with isolate PNLP_222. jackknife_history_trees.zip — posterior tree histories generated from the jackknife sensitivity analyses. jackknife_logs.zip — BEAST MCMC log files from the jackknife analyses. NS_NA_logs_trees.zip — Bayesian posterior logs and trees associated with the NS and NA analyses. Relaxed clock.zip — Bayesian analysis outputs generated under the uncorrelated lognormal relaxed molecular-clock framework. Strict clock.zip — Bayesian analysis outputs generated under the strict molecular-clock framework. shared_backbone_logs.zip — Bayesian analysis outputs associated with the shared internal-gene backbone analyses. These files complement the XML configuration files, maximum-likelihood trees, maximum-clade-credibility trees, TreeSort analyses, and Sanger chromatograms available in the associated GitHub repository. The GitHub repository contains the principal analysis inputs and organized supplementary files used throughout the study. Computational reproducibility The complete computational workflow and analysis files are organized in the associated GitHub repository: PNLP_H1N8_Pathogens — GitHub repository The GitHub repository includes the BEAST XML files for relaxed and strict molecular-clock analyses, jackknife analyses, shared-backbone analyses, TreeSort input/output files, ML trees, MCC trees, and Sanger chromatograms. The large BEAST MCMC log and posterior tree archives are additionally deposited in this OSF project to facilitate access to the complete computational outputs of the study.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to characterize the genomic features and evolutionary history of a reassortant low-pathogenic avian influenza virus (LPAI) subtype H1N8 isolated from a White-rumped Sandpiper in southern Brazil. This research is important due to the increasing concern about avian influenza viruses and their potential to reassort and evolve into more virulent forms.
Design and population
The study involved genomic analyses of an avian influenza virus isolated from a White-rumped Sandpiper sampled at Parque Nacional da Lagoa do Peixe (PNLP), southern Brazil. The research focused on the evolutionary history and reassortment patterns of the virus.
Core methods
The methods included genomic characterization using computational tools, with the archived files containing raw sequencing data, posterior tree histories, BEAST MCMC log files, and Bayesian analysis outputs for various frameworks such as relaxed and strict molecular-clock analyses, jackknife analyses, and shared-backbone analyses.
Main results
The genomic analyses identified a mosaic genome composed of gene segments associated with different South American and North American avian influenza lineages.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings highlight the genetic diversity and reassortment potential of avian influenza viruses, which may have implications for their evolution and potential zoonotic transmission.
Scientific and public-health significance
The results contribute to understanding the genetic dynamics of avian influenza viruses, which is crucial for surveillance, diagnosis, and prevention strategies to mitigate the risk of zoonotic transmission and emergence of new viral strains.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to computational and genomic analyses, with no direct clinical or epidemiological outcomes reported. The evidence is moderate as it relies on supplementary data and computational methods without detailed experimental validation.

鸡中免疫应答的表观遗传调控:机制、宿主-病原体相互作用及新兴基因组技术

Epigenetic Regulation of Immune Responses in Chickens: Mechanisms, Host–Pathogen Interactions, and Emerging Genomic Technologies
作者： Muhammad Naeem
摘要中文翻译表观遗传调控已成为调控家禽免疫功能的基本机制,通过整合遗传、环境、营养和微生物信号,而不改变DNA序列。表观基因组学的最新进展揭示了DNA甲基化、组蛋白修饰、染色质重塑和非编码RNA与免疫和炎症反应、炎症信号传导、免疫细胞分化以及宿主-病原体相互作用的关联。针对家禽的特定研究,主要在鸡中进行,已识别出与感染和挑战相关的表观遗传变化,包括禽流感、马立克氏病和细菌性炎症刺激模型,其中目前最强的证据与先天性和炎症性免疫调控相关。环境因素如营养、肠道微生物群、热应激和发育条件通过表观遗传编程进一步塑造免疫能力,而新兴证据表明,某些表观遗传修饰可能有助于跨代疾病抗性。新兴证据表明,某些表观遗传特征可能在世代间持续存在;然而,关于家禽中免疫相关表观遗传特征稳定跨代遗传的有力证据仍有限。全基因组基因组亚硫酸氢盐测序、ATAC-seq、ChIP-seq和单细胞多组学技术的进步正在改善对鸟类表观基因组的表征,而基于CRISPR的表观基因组编辑、人工智能辅助预测、表观遗传选择以及表观基因组指导的疫苗优化仍属于未来应用,需要大量的功能和现场验证。本综述总结了当前关于调控家禽免疫的表观遗传机制的知识,评估了它们在家禽中宿主-病原体相互作用和环境适应中的作用,并突出了可能促进免疫表观遗传学发现转化为改善家禽健康、疾病抗性和可持续生产的实际策略的新兴基因组技术。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在探讨表观遗传调控在家禽免疫应答中的作用,特别是在禽流感背景下,以填补非遗传因素如何影响免疫功能和宿主-病原体相互作用的知识空白。
设计与对象
本研究是对现有文献的综述,重点关注鸡中的禽流感,考察与免疫和炎症反应以及家禽特异性模型中宿主-病原体相互作用相关的表观遗传变化。
核心方法
综述利用全基因组基因组亚硫酸氢盐测序、ATAC-seq、ChIP-seq和单细胞多组学技术来表征鸟类表观基因组,同时讨论了基于CRISPR的表观基因组编辑、人工智能辅助预测、表观遗传选择以及表观基因组指导的疫苗优化等未来应用。
主要结果
综述发现,DNA甲基化、组蛋白修饰、染色质重塑和非编码RNA与禽流感模型中的免疫和炎症反应、免疫细胞分化以及宿主-病原体相互作用相关。
解释与创新
作者将这些发现解释为强调表观遗传机制在塑造家禽免疫能力和疾病抗性中的重要性,其新颖之处在于将新兴基因组技术整合到免疫表观遗传学的研究中。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可以为制定基于表观基因组的策略以改善家禽健康、疾病抗性和可持续生产提供信息,其潜在影响还可能涉及对动物和人类背景下禽流感的理解和管理。
局限与证据强度
该研究的局限性在于其依赖现有文献,且缺乏关于家禽中免疫相关表观遗传特征稳定跨代遗传的有力证据,导致其证据评估为中等可信度。

Epigenetic Regulation of Immune Responses in Chickens: Mechanisms, Host–Pathogen Interactions, and Emerging Genomic Technologies

Authors: Muhammad Naeem
Original abstract (English)
Epigenetic regulation has emerged as a fundamental mechanism governing immune function in poultry by integrating genetic, environmental, nutritional, and microbial signals without altering the DNA sequence. Recent advances in epigenomics have revealed that DNA methylation, histone modifications, chromatin remodeling, and non-coding RNAs are associated with regulation of immune and inflammatory responses, inflammatory signaling, immune-cell differentiation, and host–pathogen interactions. Poultry-specific studies, predominantly conducted in chickens, have identified infection- and challenge-associated epigenetic changes in models including avian influenza, Marek’s disease, and bacterial inflammatory stimulation, with the strongest current evidence relating to innate and inflammatory immune regulation. Environmental factors such as nutrition, gut microbiota, heat stress, and developmental conditions further shape immune competence through epigenetic programming, while emerging evidence suggests that some epigenetic modifications may contribute to transgenerational disease resistance. Emerging evidence suggests that some epigenetic signatures may persist across generations; however, robust evidence for stable transgenerational inheritance of immune-related epigenetic traits in poultry remains limited. Advances in whole-genome bisulfite sequencing, ATAC-seq, ChIP-seq, and single-cell multi-omics are improving characterization of the avian epigenome, whereas CRISPR-based epigenome editing, artificial intelligence-assisted prediction, epigenetic selection, and epigenome-informed vaccine optimization remain prospective applications requiring substantial functional and field validation. This review synthesizes current knowledge of epigenetic mechanisms regulating poultry immunity, evaluates their roles in host–pathogen interactions and environmental adaptation, and highlights emerging genomic technologies that may facilitate translation of immunoepigenetic discoveries into practical strategies for improving poultry health, disease resilience, and sustainable production.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to explore the role of epigenetic regulation in immune responses of poultry, particularly in the context of avian influenza, to address the knowledge gap regarding how non-genetic factors influence immune function and host-pathogen interactions.
Design and population
The study is a review of existing literature focusing on avian influenza in chickens, examining epigenetic changes associated with immune and inflammatory responses, as well as host-pathogen interactions in poultry-specific models.
Core methods
The review utilized advances in whole-genome bisulfite sequencing, ATAC-seq, ChIP-seq, and single-cell multi-omics to characterize the avian epigenome, while prospective applications such as CRISPR-based epigenome editing, artificial intelligence-assisted prediction, epigenetic selection, and epigenome-informed vaccine optimization were discussed.
Main results
The review found that DNA methylation, histone modifications, chromatin remodeling, and non-coding RNAs are associated with immune and inflammatory responses, immune-cell differentiation, and host–pathogen interactions in avian influenza models.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted these findings as highlighting the importance of epigenetic mechanisms in shaping immune competence and disease resistance in poultry, with the novelty being the integration of emerging genomic technologies into the study of immunoepigenetics.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform the development of epigenome-informed strategies for improving poultry health, disease resilience, and sustainable production, with potential implications for understanding and managing avian influenza in both animal and human contexts.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on existing literature and the lack of robust evidence for stable transgenerational inheritance of immune-related epigenetic traits in poultry, resulting in a moderate confidence assessment.

One Health

One Health
作者： Barry L. Johnson, Maureen Yvette Lichtveld
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在探讨One Health的综合框架,强调人类、动物、植物和生态系统健康的相互依赖关系,特别是在人兽共患病出现及其对公共卫生的影响的背景下。
设计与对象
本章对One Health的历史发展进行了全面回顾,分析了其在各级的政策格局以及阻碍其有效实施的差距,重点关注A型病毒及其相关人兽共患病。
核心方法
本章调查了美国在人类健康、动物健康、植物健康和生态系统保护方面的政策,重点分析了One Health相关性的驱动因素,包括人兽共患病的出现、气候变化、土地利用变化、抗菌药物耐药性和生物多样性丧失。
主要结果
The chapter highlights case studies on H5N1 in dairy cattle and poultry, as well as wastewater surveillance for COVID-19, to illustrate both progress and shortcomings in cross-sector coordination.
解释与创新
作者将研究结果解释为表明需要加强跨部门合作,并识别出关键政策差距,例如数据系统隔离以及高收入国家与低收入和中等收入国家之间投资的差异。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可以为制定更加综合的监测和政策框架提供信息,以应对人兽共患病风险并增强公共卫生准备能力。
局限与证据强度
证据基于现有政策和案例研究的回顾,这可能限制分析的深度。由于依赖报告数据而非实证测量,对政策差距的评估是中等的。

One Health

Authors: Barry L. Johnson, Maureen Yvette Lichtveld
Original abstract (English)
One Health is an integrative framework that recognizes the functional interdependence of human, animal, plant, and ecosystem health. This chapter examines the concept&s;s historical evolution; its policy landscape at the city, state, national, and international levels; and the ongoing gaps that limit effective implementation. Beginning with foundational definitions and the drivers of One Health relevance, including zoonotic disease emergence, climate change, land-use change, antimicrobial resistance, and biodiversity loss, the chapter surveys U.S. policies across human health, animal health, plant health, and ecosystem protection. Case studies on H5N1 in dairy cattle and poultry and COVID-19 wastewater surveillance illustrate both progress and shortcomings in cross-sector coordination. Occupational health risks at the human–animal interface are also addressed. Key policy gaps identified include siloed data systems, uneven investment in One Health workforce development, lagging environmental surveillance, and disparities between high-income countries and low- and middle-income countries. The chapter concludes by highlighting emerging opportunities, including regional consortia and national academy initiatives, to accelerate intersectoral collaboration and build a more resilient One Health policy framework.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to explore the integrative framework of One Health, emphasizing the interdependence of human, animal, plant, and ecosystem health, particularly in the context of zoonotic disease emergence and its implications for public health.
Design and population
The chapter provides a comprehensive review of the historical evolution of One Health, its policy landscape at various levels, and the gaps that hinder its effective implementation, focusing on avian influenza A viruses and related zoonotic infections.
Core methods
The chapter surveys U.S. policies across human health, animal health, plant health, and ecosystem protection, with a focus on the drivers of One Health relevance, including zoonotic disease emergence, climate change, land-use change, antimicrobial resistance, and biodiversity loss.
Main results
The chapter highlights case studies on H5N1 in dairy cattle and poultry, as well as wastewater surveillance for COVID-19, to illustrate both progress and shortcomings in cross-sector coordination.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret the findings as demonstrating the need for improved intersectoral collaboration and the identification of key policy gaps, such as siloed data systems and disparities in investment between high-income and low- and middle-income countries.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform the development of more integrated surveillance and policy frameworks to address zoonotic disease risks and enhance public health preparedness.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence is based on a review of existing policies and case studies, which may limit the depth of analysis. The assessment of policy gaps is moderate due to the reliance on reported data rather than empirical measurements.

使用卫星影像对南乔治亚岛南部象海豹 Mirounga leonina 进行更新的种群普查

An updated census of southern elephant seals Mirounga leonina at South Georgia using satellite imagery
作者： Connor C. G. Bamford, Ellen Bowler, Matthew Gascoyne, Jamie Coleman, Nathan Fenney, Cameron Fox-Clarke, M. A. Fedak, Luis Hückstädt, Philip Hollyman, Jaimie Cleeland
摘要中文翻译历史上,南乔治亚岛曾支持全球最大的南部象海豹 Mirounga leonina 繁殖种群。然而自1995年以来,该种群一直未被充分评估,被认为处于稳定状态。本文提供了对该南大洋种群的更新评估,并展示了亚米级分辨率卫星影像作为一种有价值的方法,用于对广泛、偏远且具有挑战性的繁殖种群进行普查。在2024和2025两个繁殖季节中,研究人员使用分辨率为0.3米至0.5米的卫星影像,并在广义加法框架下将基于影像的计数与外推建模相结合,以估算未观测海滩上的种群数量。通过这种方法,我们估计南乔治亚岛有107,630(95% CI: 93,945 – 123,342)只繁殖雌性,与1995年的历史基准值113,444(se = 4,902)相比。这对应于总种群数量为376,705只个体(95% CI: 328,808 – 431,697)。然而,这不应被解释为种群稳定的证据。本次普查是在高致病性禽流感在南大洋传播之后进行的,因此反映了近期受到干扰的种群状况,而调查方法之间的差异以及卫星校正框架的有限验证也影响了与1995年普查的直接比较。鉴于国际自然保护联盟(IUCN)最近将南部象海豹重新评估为易危物种,我们的研究结果强调了在南乔治亚岛重新进行种群评估的重要性,并突显了基于卫星的方法在追踪偏远南大洋繁殖地种群动态方面的价值。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在解决自1995年以来南乔治亚岛南部象海豹缺乏近期评估的问题,因为该种群被认为处于稳定状态。研究旨在利用先进的卫星影像技术提供更新的种群普查数据。
设计与对象
本研究在2024和2025两个繁殖季节期间对南乔治亚岛的南部象海豹进行了更新的种群普查。研究重点是利用卫星影像和外推建模来估算种群规模。
核心方法
研究人员使用亚米级分辨率的卫星影像,并在广义加法框架下将基于影像的计数与外推建模相结合,以估算未观测海滩上的南部象海豹数量。
主要结果
本研究估计南乔治亚岛有107,630(95% CI: 93,945 – 123,342)只繁殖雌性,与1995年的历史基准值113,444(se = 4,902)相比。这对应于总种群数量为376,705只个体(95% CI: 328,808 – 431,697)。
解释与创新
作者认为他们的研究结果突显了在南乔治亚岛重新进行种群评估的重要性,并强调了基于卫星的方法在追踪偏远南大洋繁殖地种群动态方面的价值。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果为监测南部象海豹种群提供了关键数据,这对保护工作以及理解近期扰动(如高致病性禽流感的传播)对生态影响具有重要意义。
局限与证据强度
本研究承认,由于调查方法之间的差异以及卫星校正框架的有限验证,研究结果不应被解释为种群稳定的证据。

An updated census of southern elephant seals Mirounga leonina at South Georgia using satellite imagery

Authors: Connor C. G. Bamford, Ellen Bowler, Matthew Gascoyne, Jamie Coleman, Nathan Fenney, Cameron Fox-Clarke, M. A. Fedak, Luis Hückstädt, Philip Hollyman, Jaimie Cleeland
Original abstract (English)
Historically, South Georgia supported the largest breeding population of southern elephant seals Mirounga leonina globally. Yet since 1995, this population has remained largely unassessed and was presumed to be stable. Here, we provide an updated assessment of this sub-Antarctic population and demonstrate the application of sub-meter resolution satellite imagery as a valuable approach for censusing an extensive, remote, and challenging breeding population. Across two breeding seasons, 2024 and 2025, we tasked satellite imagery with resolutions between 0.3 m and 0.5 m and integrated image-based counts with extrapolative modeling under a generalised additive framework to estimate abundance at unobserved beaches. Using this approach, we estimate 107,630 (95% CI: 93,945 – 123,342) breeding females at South Georgia, compared to the historical benchmark of 113,444 (se = 4,902) in 1995. This corresponds to a total population of 376,705 individuals (95% CI: 328,808 – 431,697). However, this should not be interpreted as evidence of population stability. The present census followed the spread of highly pathogenic avian influenza through the sub-Antarctic and therefore reflects a recently perturbed population, while methodological differences between surveys and limited validation of the satellite correction framework also affect direct comparability with the 1995 census. In light of the recent IUCN re-assessment of southern elephant seals as Vulnerable, our results highlight both the importance of renewed population assessment at South Georgia and the value for tracking population trajectories across remote Southern Ocean breeding sites.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the lack of recent assessments of southern elephant seals at South Georgia, which has been presumed stable since 1995. The research sought to provide an updated census using advanced satellite imagery techniques.
Design and population
The study conducted an updated census of southern elephant seals at South Georgia during the breeding seasons of 2024 and 2025. It focused on estimating the population size using satellite imagery and extrapolative modeling.
Core methods
The researchers used sub-meter resolution satellite imagery and integrated image-based counts with extrapolative modeling under a generalised additive framework to estimate the abundance of seals at unobserved beaches.
Main results
The study estimated 107,630 (95% CI: 93,945 – 123,342) breeding females at South Georgia, compared to the historical benchmark of 113,444 (se = 4,902) in 1995. This corresponds to a total population of 376,705 individuals (95% CI: 328,808 – 431,697).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted their findings as highlighting the importance of renewed population assessment at South Georgia and the value of satellite-based methods for tracking population trajectories in remote Southern Ocean breeding sites.
Scientific and public-health significance
The results provide critical data for monitoring the population of southern elephant seals, which is relevant for conservation efforts and understanding the ecological impact of recent perturbations such as the spread of highly pathogenic avian influenza.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study acknowledges that the results should not be interpreted as evidence of population stability due to methodological differences between surveys and limited validation of the satellite correction framework.

空间解析传染病模型在局部暴发缓解中的应用(以及高效的数学软件)

Spatially resolved infectious disease models for localized outbreak mitigation (and efficient mathematical software)
作者： Kühn, Martin Joachim

查看研究七要素
问题与背景
研究旨在开发空间解析传染病模型以实现局部暴发缓解,并高效数学软件,通过先进的建模技术提高大流行准备能力。
设计与对象
The study involved a simulated spread of SARS-CoV-2 cases for one initial scenario with about 18% red zones and 8 different strategies. Random initial incidence was set at 75-150 weekly cases per 100,000 individuals for 2-20% of the counties, with incidence below 10 otherwise.
核心方法
研究采用了混合代理-元种群模型,结合了焦点区域的代理模型和连接区域的ODE模型。还包括一个具有8000个代理、1%初始感染率和基于区域的不同传播率的证明概念空间混合模型。
主要结果
模拟时间30天后的中位数结果显示,空间混合模型在40,000个代理的情况下,将运行时间减少了98%,同时保持了与基于代理的模型相似的扩展行为。
解释与创新
作者解释结果表明,空间混合模型在必要时能够捕捉到随机性和个体行为的效率,同时利用ODE模型在连接区域的计算优势。这种方法代表了不同建模技术在流行病控制中的新颖整合。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果可以指导更高效和准确的模型开发,特别是在计算资源有限的场景中,支持在大流行准备中使用空间解析模型。
局限与证据强度
研究受限于对模拟数据的依赖和缺乏实地验证。证据为中等,因为它提出了一个概念模型并讨论了对未来研究和应用的含义。

Spatially resolved infectious disease models for localized outbreak mitigation (and efficient mathematical software)

Authors: Kühn, Martin Joachim

View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to develop spatially resolved infectious disease models for localized outbreak mitigation and efficient mathematical software, addressing the need for improved pandemic preparedness through advanced modeling techniques.
Design and population
The study involved a simulated spread of SARS-CoV-2 cases for one initial scenario with about 18% red zones and 8 different strategies. Random initial incidence was set at 75-150 weekly cases per 100,000 individuals for 2-20% of the counties, with incidence below 10 otherwise.
Core methods
The study employed a hybrid agent-metapopulation model, combining agent-based models in the focus region with ODE-based models for connected regions. It also included a proof-of-concept spatial hybrid model with 8000 agents, 1% initially infected, and different transmission rates based on regions.
Main results
The median result after 30 days of simulation time showed that the spatial-hybrid model reduced runtime by 98% for 40,000 agents compared to agent-based models, while maintaining similar scaling behavior.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted the results as demonstrating the efficiency of the spatial-hybrid model in capturing stochasticity and individual behavior when necessary, while leveraging the computational advantages of ODE-based models for connected regions. This approach represents a novel integration of different modeling techniques for epidemic control.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform the development of more efficient and accurate models for outbreak mitigation, particularly in scenarios with limited computational resources, and support the use of spatially resolved models in pandemic preparedness.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on simulated data and the absence of real-world validation. The evidence is moderate as it presents a proof-of-concept model and discusses implications for future research and applications.

Journal: Frontiers in Cellular and Infection Microbiology  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-09-22  |  DOI: 10.3389/fcimb.2026.1957795  |  Date basis: online_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 16
A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

牛中不同的流感界面:A(H5N1)病毒从野生鸟类到野生鸟类的反复跨物种溢出以及流感D病毒的流行性传播

Distinct influenza interfaces in cattle: repeated spillovers of highly pathogenic avian influenza A(H5N1) from wild birds and enzootic circulation of influenza D virus
作者： Siqi Wang, Kaili Gao, Lianmin Li, Yiming Chang, Luyu Mao, Shuchang Cheng, Yang Bai, Shuya Yang, Sainan Li, Yongli Guo
摘要中文翻译牛现在占据了两个流行病学上不同的流感界面。本文简要综述比较了A(H5N1)病毒和流感D病毒(IDV)在流行病学、病理学、实验-传播、基因组和监测方面的证据,探讨这两种病毒在来源生态、组织嗜性、病毒释放、传播、持续性和人兽共患病证据方面的差异。遗传上不同的H5N1病毒已反复从野生鸟类溢出到美国奶牛中。在泌乳牛中,感染主要发生在乳腺泡上皮细胞,并在生乳中产生高滴度的传染性病毒;跨牧场的牛群流动已将基因型B3.13传播至奶业生产网络。在受影响的农场中,病毒RNA在生乳和呼吸道样本中被检测到;传染性病毒可从生乳和乳腺组织中一致回收,但在呼吸道或环境样本(如挤奶厅空气和污水)中仅偶尔检测到。这些发现指出了暴露环境,但并未阐明主要的自然牛间传播途径或病毒如何到达乳腺。职业性感染已发生,但尚未证明牛的持续适应或长期储存宿主状态。相比之下,IDV是一种适应于牛的呼吸道病毒,通过直接接触和短距离空气传播维持其存在,IDV的基因组片段之间存在种间重组,并且常与牛呼吸道疾病综合征(BRDC)相关,尽管其在BRDC中的独立致病作用尚未被证实。人类血清学反应和在人类气道模型中的高效复制表明了暴露和生物学兼容性,但并未确认人类的活跃感染或进一步的传播。这些对比支持针对H5N1的事件驱动、以牛奶为中心的调查,以及针对IDV的纵向呼吸道和基因组监测调查,这些调查建立在共享的One Health诊断、测序、流行病学和职业健康基础设施之上。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本文简要综述比较了A(H5N1)病毒和流感D病毒(IDV)在流行病学、病理学、实验-传播、基因组和监测方面的证据,探讨这两种病毒在来源生态、组织嗜性、病毒释放、传播、持续性和人兽共患病证据方面的差异。
证据基础与方法
这些对比支持针对H5N1的事件驱动、以牛奶为中心的调查,以及针对IDV的纵向呼吸道和基因组监测调查,这些调查建立在共享的One Health诊断、测序、流行病学和职业健康基础设施之上。
共识与结论
职业性感染已发生,但尚未证明牛的持续适应或长期储存宿主状态。
争议与缺口
相比之下,IDV是一种适应于牛的呼吸道病毒,通过直接接触和短距离空气传播维持其存在,IDV的基因组片段之间存在种间重组,并且常与牛呼吸道疾病综合征(BRDC)相关,尽管其在BRDC中的独立致病作用尚未被证实。
科研与实践启示
人类血清学反应和在人类气道模型中的高效复制表明了暴露和生物学兼容性,但并未确认人类的活跃感染或进一步的传播。

Distinct influenza interfaces in cattle: repeated spillovers of highly pathogenic avian influenza A(H5N1) from wild birds and enzootic circulation of influenza D virus

Authors: Siqi Wang, Kaili Gao, Lianmin Li, Yiming Chang, Luyu Mao, Shuchang Cheng, Yang Bai, Shuya Yang, Sainan Li, Yongli Guo
Original abstract (English)
Cattle now occupy two epidemiologically distinct influenza interfaces. This Mini Review comparatively synthesizes epidemiological, pathological, experimental-transmission, genomic, and surveillance evidence for highly pathogenic avian influenza A(H5N1) and influenza D virus (IDV), asking how source ecology, tissue tropism, shedding, transmission, persistence, and zoonotic evidence differ between the two viruses. Genetically distinct H5N1 viruses have spilled over repeatedly from wild birds into United States dairy cattle. In lactating cows, infection is prominent in mammary alveolar epithelium and produces high titers of infectious virus in raw milk; inter-herd cattle movements have disseminated genotype B3.13 through dairy production networks. On affected farms, viral RNA has been detected in milk and respiratory specimens; infectious virus is recovered consistently from raw milk and mammary tissues but only sporadically from respiratory or environmental samples such as milking-parlour air and wastewater. These findings identify exposure settings but do not resolve the dominant natural cow-to-cow transmission route or how the virus reaches the mammary gland. Occupational infections have occurred, but sustained cattle adaptation or long-term reservoir status has not been demonstrated. In contrast, IDV is a cattle-adapted respiratory virus maintained through direct contact and short-range airborne transmission, with interlineage reassortment among IDV genome segments and frequent association with bovine respiratory disease complex (BRDC), although an independent causal role in BRDC remains unproven. Human seroreactivity and efficient replication in human airway models indicate exposure and biological compatibility, but not confirmed productive human infection or onward transmission. These contrasts support event-triggered, milk-centered investigation for H5N1 and longitudinal respiratory and genomic surveillance for IDV, built on shared One Health diagnostic, sequencing, epidemiological, and occupational-health infrastructure.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This Mini Review comparatively synthesizes epidemiological, pathological, experimental-transmission, genomic, and surveillance evidence for highly pathogenic avian influenza A(H5N1) and influenza D virus (IDV), asking how source ecology, tissue tropism, shedding, transmission, persistence, and zoonotic evidence differ between the two viruses.
Evidence base and method
These contrasts support event-triggered, milk-centered investigation for H5N1 and longitudinal respiratory and genomic surveillance for IDV, built on shared One Health diagnostic, sequencing, epidemiological, and occupational-health infrastructure.
Consensus and conclusions
Occupational infections have occurred, but sustained cattle adaptation or long-term reservoir status has not been demonstrated.
Controversies and gaps
In contrast, IDV is a cattle-adapted respiratory virus maintained through direct contact and short-range airborne transmission, with interlineage reassortment among IDV genome segments and frequent association with bovine respiratory disease complex (BRDC), although an independent causal role in BRDC remains unproven.
Research and practice implications
Human seroreactivity and efficient replication in human airway models indicate exposure and biological compatibility, but not confirmed productive human infection or onward transmission.

Journal: Journal of Virology  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-09-23  |  DOI: 10.1128/jvi.01265-26  |  Date basis: online_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, e0126526

流感血凝素侧翼区的抗原性和结构分析

Antigenic and structural analysis of the influenza hemagglutinin lateral patch
作者： Jordan I. Lemus-Reyes, Monica L. Fernández‐Quintero, Edgardo Ayala, Olivia M. Swanson, Marina R. Good, Wei Ji, Devika Suja, Julianna Han, Andrew B. Ward, Jenna J. Guthmiller

‘值得关注’禽流感研究结果引发‘关键’监测呼吁 - 兽医时报

‘Concerning’ bird flu study findings prompt ‘essential’ surveillance call - Vet Times
新闻简报一项于2026年2月11日发表在《自然·通讯》上的研究发现,H5N1禽流感病毒在美国奶牛中出现了值得关注的适应性变化,这些奶牛自2024年以来已感染该病毒。研究确定了突变,如PB2 M631L和PA K497R,这些变化增强了病毒在牛和人细胞中的复制能力,表明其正在积极适应哺乳动物。这些突变似乎对禽类复制几乎没有或没有负面影响,这引发了对病毒向人类传播潜力的担忧。截至报告发布时,已有19个州的1,084个牧场受到影响,出现71例人类病例和2例死亡。该研究警告称,病毒的持续进化可能增加人类感染的风险,从而引发对紧急监测、生物安全措施以及疫苗开发的迫切呼吁。尽管该病毒目前在人与人之间传播效率不高,但研究结果强调了在病毒适应家畜的过程中保持警惕的重要性,因为家畜与人类接触率较高。
查看新闻五要素
时间
研究结果于2026年2月11日发布,该病毒自2024年起在美国奶牛群中传播。事件日期为2024年,自那时起持续进行监测和研究。
地点与对象
该研究聚焦于美国,特别是19个州的奶牛群,以及接触这些奶牛群的人类群体。受影响的人群包括奶牛和人类。
事件
一项研究分析了H5N1病毒B3.13基因型,发现其在奶牛中出现了值得关注的适应性变化,增强了在牛和人细胞中的复制能力。研究结果表明,该病毒正在积极适应哺乳动物,增加了向人类传播的潜在风险。
规模、影响与风险
截至报告发布,19个州的1,084个牧场已确认出现H5N1病毒在奶牛中的感染,共有71例人类病例,导致2例死亡。该研究指出,病毒可能进一步适应,从而增加人类感染的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究人员呼吁采取紧急监测和生物安全措施以控制动物中的流感。他们强调需要推进疫苗的研发和测试,研究结果被描述为值得关注,但尚未表明存在高效的传播人传人传播。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

‘Concerning’ bird flu study findings prompt ‘essential’ surveillance call - Vet Times

News Brief
A study published in Nature Communications on 11 Feb 2026 found concerning adaptations of the H5N1 bird flu virus in US dairy cattle, which have been infected since 2024. The research identified mutations such as PB2 M631L and PA K497R that enhance the virus's replication in bovine and human cells, suggesting it is actively adapting to mammals. These mutations appear to have little or no negative impact on avian replication, raising concerns about potential spillover to humans. As of the report, 1,084 herds in 19 states have been affected, with 71 human cases and two deaths. The study warns that continued viral evolution could increase the risk of human transmission, prompting calls for urgent surveillance, biosecurity measures, and vaccine development. While the virus currently does not transmit efficiently between humans, the findings highlight the need for vigilance as it adapts to domestic animals, which have high human contact rates.
Original source text (English)
11 Feb 2026 ‘Concerning’ bird flu study findings prompt ‘essential’ surveillance call A study examined the B3.13 genotype of H5N1, which has been circulating in US dairy herds since 2024. Cattle in Texas. Image: Kay A/peopleimages.com / Adobe Stock Researchers have stressed the need for ongoing surveillance after two studies tracking avian influenza’s ability to adapt to other species returned “concerning” results. One study, published in Nature Communications, examined the B3.13 genotype of H5N1, which has been circulating in US dairy herds since 2024. Researchers discovered several mammalian adaptations in cattle, enhancing replication in bovine and human cells. Virus sequences One such mutation (PB2 M631L) was found in all cattle virus sequences studied, while another – PA K497R – appeared in around 95%. The former was described as “the key adaptive mutation” that allowed the B3.13 genotype to efficiently replicate in cattle. Further mutations, PB2 E627K and PB2 D740N, were said to indicate ongoing adaptation of H5N1 viruses to further optimise polymerase function in cattle. Both appeared to have little or no negative impact on replication in birds, suggesting the mutations could be maintained if the viruses spill back into avian species. The study concluded that, “in the absence of an effective control strategy, the high pathogenicity H5N1 virus may now become endemic in US dairy cattle” and that urgent vaccine development and testing is a “priority”. ‘Zoonotic spillover’ Thomas Peacock, co-corresponding author and fellow at The Pirbright Institute, said: “Our results show the circulation of H5N1 in dairy cattle is actively driving viral adaptation to mammals. This improves the virus’ ability to replicate in cattle and heightens the risk of zoonotic spillover. “Infections in humans linked to the cattle outbreak have so far been mild and limited, but the findings are concerning. The adapted cattle virus replicates efficiently in human cells, retains the ability to infect birds and swine, and shows no clear fitness cost that would prevent it from spreading between species. “While current evidence suggests it does not yet transmit efficiently between humans, continued exposure and viral evolution increase the risk of further adaptations that could change this.” Since it first emerged in cattle in the US in 2024, 1,084 herds in 19 states have had confirmed cases, and there have also been 71 human cases resulting in two deaths. Eighty viruses In the other study, also published in Nature Communications, researchers examined a panel of more than 80 viruses, representing 15 distinct H5Nx genotypes spanning 60 years of evolution. They discovered some of the more recent clade 2.3.4.4b H5N1 viruses are better able to infect and replicate in bovine cells and tissues than some older variants, and that the spread of such viruses in mammals increases the pool of variants carrying genetic traits that counteract an important human barrier against zoonotic influenza. Overall, they said their findings “suggest the zoonotic potential of H5N1 2.3.4.4b is relatively high”. Pablo Murcia, of the MRC–University of Glasgow Centre for Virus Research, a co-author on both papers, said: “Influenza viruses are constantly changing and this rapid evolution allows them to infect new animal species. “If the viruses start spreading among domestic animals, the chances of zoonotic infections increase, given the high contact rate of humans with domestic animals. The more the viruses adapt to mammals, the more likely is that they might adapt to humans. “Surveillance and biosecurity measures are essential to control influenza in animals.”
View five news elements
Time
The study findings were reported on 11 Feb 2026, and the virus has been circulating in US dairy herds since 2024. The event date is 2024, with ongoing surveillance and research since then.
Location and population
The study focused on the United States, specifically dairy herds in 19 states, and human populations exposed to these herds. The affected population includes cattle and humans.
Event
A study examining the B3.13 genotype of H5N1 found concerning adaptations in cattle, enhancing replication in bovine and human cells. The findings suggest the virus is actively adapting to mammals, increasing the risk of zoonotic spillover.
Scale, impact and risk
As of the report, 1,084 herds in 19 states have had confirmed cases of H5N1 in cattle, and there have been 71 human cases resulting in two deaths. The study highlights the potential for further adaptations that could increase the risk of human transmission.
Response, status and uncertainty
Researchers have called for urgent surveillance and biosecurity measures to control influenza in animals. They emphasized the need for vaccine development and testing, and the findings are described as concerning but not yet indicating efficient human-to-human transmission.

FFAR研究测试雾化方法以减少禽流感 传播 - 《篱笆桩》

FFAR research to test fogging approach to reduce avian influenza transmission - The Fence Post
新闻简报食品与农业研究基金会(FFAR)和Entomol Products,LLC正在通过一项ROAR资助计划投资448,940美元,测试使用氢化猫薄荷油(HCO)的雾化方法以减少 poultry housing 中的 禽流感 传播。这项研究由Jasmine Bruno博士和Ekramy Sayedahmed博士领导,旨在评估HCO在低浓度下是否能有效破坏空气和表面中的禽流感病毒。禽流感,特别是H5N1,对美国家禽和鸡蛋产业造成重大经济损失,该病毒在鸟类中传播迅速,导致受感染鸡群大规模淘汰。该研究将评估HCO对低致病性禽流感的影响,以期为高致病性毒株的潜在应用提供信息。目前的研究结果尚属初步,旨在开发一种基于科学的干预措施,以增强国家对禽流感 暴发的应对能力,并保护家禽生产者的生计。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究作为一项2026年资助的项目进行,发表日期为2026年9月23日。
地点与对象
该研究聚焦于美国家禽和鸡蛋产业,特别是在禽流感存在风险的地区。
事件
FFAR和Entomol Products,LLC正在测试一种使用氢化猫薄荷油(HCO)的雾化方法,以减少 poultry housing 中的 禽流感 传播。
规模、影响与风险
禽流感对美国家禽和鸡蛋产业造成严重经济损失,该病毒在鸟类中传播迅速,导致受感染鸡群大规模淘汰。该研究旨在开发一种实际的干预措施,以减轻这些影响。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究处于测试阶段,目标是确定HCO的有效气溶胶输送参数。目前的研究结果尚属初步,旨在为潜在的生物安全工具应用提供信息。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

FFAR research to test fogging approach to reduce avian influenza transmission - The Fence Post

News Brief
The Foundation for Food & Agriculture Research (FFAR) and Entomol Products, LLC are investing $448,940 in a ROAR grant to test a fogging approach using hydrogenated catmint oil (HCO) to reduce avian influenza transmission in poultry housing. This research, led by Dr. Jasmine Bruno and Dr. Ekramy Sayedahmed, aims to evaluate whether HCO can effectively disrupt avian influenza viruses in the air and on surfaces at low concentrations. Avian influenza, particularly H5N1, poses significant economic risks to the U.S. poultry and egg industries, with the virus spreading rapidly among birds and causing mass depopulation. The study will assess the antiviral effects of HCO against low pathogenic avian influenza to inform its potential use against highly pathogenic strains. The findings are preliminary and seek to develop a science-driven intervention to strengthen national readiness for avian influenza outbreaks and protect poultry producers' livelihoods.
Original source text (English)
Breaking News - Your Trusted Source for Ag News & Information Subscribe News State or Region Wyoming Nebraska Kansas Colorado Farming & Ranching Western Heritage Events Ag & Politics People in Ag Obituaries Celebrations Opinion Our Editor Reader Submissions Baxter Black Lee Pitts Mad Jack Hanks Milo Yield Mr. Truck Rangeviews Sow’s Ear Industry Info Auctions Ag Event Calendar Production Sale Reports Market Reports Cavvy Savvy Entertainment Ag Event Calendar Recipes Art, Photos & Poetry Auction Calendar Feature Magazines AgriCULTURE Breeder’s Connection Homeland Forage Open Season Summer Events Country Christmas Classifieds Employment Rentals Real Estate Farm & Ranch Service Directory All Classifieds Place an Ad Newsletters Subscribe News State or Region Farming & Ranching Western Heritage Events Ag & Politics People in Ag Obituaries Celebrations Opinion Our Editor Reader Submissions Baxter Black Lee Pitts Mad Jack Hanks Milo Yield Mr. Truck Rangeviews Sow’s Ear Industry Info Auctions Ag Event Calendar Production Sale Reports Market Reports Cavvy Savvy Entertainment Ag Event Calendar Recipes Art, Photos & Poetry Auction Calendar Feature Magazines AgriCULTURE Breeder’s Connection Homeland Forage Open Season Summer Events Country Christmas Classifieds Employment Rentals Real Estate Farm & Ranch Service Directory All Classifieds Place an Ad Newsletters Subscribe About Us| Contact Us| Submissions| Advertise| Pick up a Copy| 67° YOUR AD HERE » FFAR research to test fogging approach to reduce avian influenza transmission NewsNews | Sep 23, 2026 Foundation for Food & Agriculture Research Share this story Share Share on Facebook Share on Twitter Share on LinkedIn Share on WhatsApp Share via Email Share via SMS Copy Link A A INDIANAPOLIS, Ind. — H5N1 influenza, commonly known as bird flu, continues to threaten U.S. poultry and egg production. The Foundation for Food & Agriculture Research and Entomol Products, LLC are investing $448,940 in a Rapid Outcomes from Agricultural Research or ROAR grant to test an innovative, plant-based approach to reduce transmission of avian influenza. Researchers are determining whether intermittently fogging or misting hydrogenated catmint oil (HCO), a product with demonstrated antiviral properties, in poultry housing can interrupt transmission of avian influenza viruses. Avian influenza has severe economic consequences for the U.S. poultry and egg industries. The virus spreads rapidly among birds and leads to mass depopulation of infected flocks. This influenza virus is classified as high or low pathogenic avian influenza based on how it affects poultry. Highly pathogenic avian influenza viruses cause severe disease and high mortality in infected poultry, while low pathogenic avian influenza is a mild or asymptomatic viral infection. “Producers need practical solutions to reduce avian influenza transmission that work with existing infrastructure to protect their flocks and limit losses,” said Dr. Jasmine Bruno, FFAR scientific program director. “As fogging is a widely used practice in poultry houses, we want to know if it’s a promising mechanism for protecting birds.” TESTING HCO HCO is a modified plant essential oil and an EPA-registered active ingredient used in commercially available insect repellents. Previous research found that aerosolized formulations containing HCO significantly reduced H1N1 influenza virus in the air. Led by Dr. David Hallahan, chief science officer at Entomol, and Dr. Ekramy Sayedahmed, assistant professor of poultry medicine at Purdue University, researchers are testing whether HCO can reduce avian influenza transmission. The researchers are using low pathogenic avian influenza to evaluate HCO’s antiviral effects against avian influenza and inform its potential application against highly pathogenic avian influenza. The team is also establishing effective aerosol delivery parameters, including HCO concentration, fogging duration and frequency, effective range and overall efficacy. “Entomol’s hydrogenated catmint oil technology has demonstrated the ability to disrupt influenza viruses in the air and on surfaces at very low concentrations, said Dr. Hallahan. “This FFAR-supported project will allow us to rigorously evaluate its potential as a practical biosecurity tool for poultry producers.” FFAR is investing in this research to support the U.S. Department of Agriculture’s research priority on increasing profitability of farmers and ranchers. This research aims to develop a practical, science-driven intervention that strengthens national readiness for avian influenza outbreaks and protects U.S. poultry producers’ livelihoods. FFAR’s ROAR program rapidly funds research and outreach in response to emerging or unanticipated threats to the U.S. food supply or agricultural systems. 澳大利亚H5N1禽流感病毒跨物种溢出至海洋哺乳动物和野生动物 - 新闻 - Mongabay

H5N1 bird flu jumps to marine mammals and wildlife in Australia - news - Mongabay
新闻简报H5N1禽流感病毒在澳大利亚的海洋哺乳动物和野生动物中被检测到,这标志着对生物多样性的重大担忧。2026年8月下旬,该病毒在南澳大利亚阿德莱德的一只红狐中被发现;2026年9月,该病毒在一只澳大利亚海狮和一只普通海豚中被确认。这些病例与感染的野生鸟类物种有关,包括小企鹅、乌鸦、冠 tern 和鹈鹕。研究人员警告称,病毒在鸟类繁殖季节的传播预计会加速,并且该病毒可能侵入哺乳动物的中枢神经系统,导致严重的脑部炎症。南澳大利亚当局正在监测这一情况,强调需要清除感染动物的尸体以防止进一步的传播。虽然目前尚无明确的方法可以阻止病毒的传播,但正在努力限制其对生态系统和野生动物种群的影响。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年8月下旬至9月
地点与对象
南澳大利亚,包括弗卢里乌半岛和约克半岛,受影响的野生动物包括小企鹅、乌鸦、冠 tern、鹈鹕、红狐、澳大利亚海狮和普通海豚。
事件
H5N1禽流感病毒在澳大利亚的海洋哺乳动物和野生动物中被检测到,包括2026年8月阿德莱德的红狐,以及2026年9月的澳大利亚海狮和普通海豚。病毒的传播归因于感染的野生鸟类物种,由州当局和研究人员报告。
规模、影响与风险
H5N1病毒已在多种野生动物中被确认,包括濒危的澳大利亚海狮。该病毒可能侵入哺乳动物的中枢神经系统,导致严重的脑部炎症。研究人员警告称,该病毒可能影响整个生态系统。
应对、状态与不确定性
当局正在监测病毒的传播,并已确认海洋哺乳动物和野生动物中的病例。应对措施包括清除感染动物的尸体以减少病毒的储存宿主。目前该情况仍在调查中,尚无明确的方法可以阻止病毒的传播。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

H5N1 bird flu jumps to marine mammals and wildlife in Australia - news - Mongabay

News Brief
H5N1 bird flu has been detected in marine mammals and wildlife in Australia, marking a significant concern for biodiversity. In late August 2026, the virus was found in a red fox in Adelaide, South Australia, and in September 2026, it was confirmed in an Australian sea lion and a common dolphin. These cases are linked to infected wild bird species, including little penguins, magpies, crested terns, and pelicans. The spread is expected to accelerate during bird breeding seasons, with researchers warning that the virus can invade a mammal’s central nervous system, causing severe brain inflammation. South Australian authorities are monitoring the situation, emphasizing the need to remove infected carcasses to prevent further transmission. While there is no definitive way to stop the spread, efforts are being made to limit its impact on ecosystems and wildlife populations.
Original source text (English)
Features Videos Podcasts Specials Articles Shorts Donate English Español (Spanish) Français (French) Bahasa Indonesia (Indonesian) Brasil (Portuguese) India (English) हिंदी (Hindi) বাংলা (Bengali) Swahili Videos Podcasts Articles Short News Feature Stories The Latest Explore All H5N1 bird flu jumps to marine mammals and wildlife in Australia Naina Rao 21 Sep 2026 Australia Comments Share article Share this article If you liked this story, share it with other people. Facebook Linkedin Threads Whatsapp Reddit Email Page link H5N1 avian influenza is spreading rapidly across southeastern Australia owing to infected wild bird species, including little penguins, magpies, crested terns and pelicans. Bird aggregations during breeding seasons will likely accelerate transmission to mammals, said Victoria’s chief biodiversity officer James Todd. In late August, H5N1 jumped to a mammal, a red fox (Vulpes vulpes), in Adelaide, South Australia. Acting chief veterinary officer Cameron Bell said that while wild foxes generally do not spread the virus directly, scavenging pests risk moving infected carcasses across habitats, according to media reports. In September, state authorities confirmed H5N1 in an Australian sea lion (Neophoca cinerea) and a common dolphin (Delphinus delphis). South Australian Environment Minister Emily Bourke said that the sea lion — a critically endangered species currently declining by 2% a year — was recovering from a shark attack when it succumbed to the virus, reports ABC Australia. Cetacean researcher Mike Bossley is concerned about how quickly a dolphin contracted the disease. In a news report, he called it both “shocking ... and very worrying.” Bourke added that South Australia has recorded bird flu in another mammal species, long-nosed fur seals (Arctocephalus forsteri) and confirmed the nation’s first pelican case on the Yorke Peninsula. “The first confirmed H5N1 death recorded for [the Australian sea lion] in Australia marks a significant and worrying moment for Australian wildlife,” Bill Bateman, associate professor of behavioral ecology at Curtin University, Australia, wrote in The Conversation. He added that the evolving H5N1 strain can invade a mammal’s central nervous system, causing severe brain inflammation. The way marine mammals contract the virus is likely from birds, Bateman wrote. Sick or dead seabirds can infect coastal breeding grounds, he added, either when animals eat them or come into contact with bodily fluids or contaminated water and substrate. While there’s no “real way” to stop the spread of H5N1 now that it’s here, Bateman noted that there are ways to reduce its impact. There is a way to limit exposure, he said. “Removing carcasses safely is crucial, as it removes these viral reservoirs, and stops it getting into Australia’s many scavenging animals.” Along South Australia’s Fleurieu Peninsula, volunteers with the Victor Harbor Dolphin Watch Group are on the lookout for dolphins exhibiting abnormal swimming or neurological symptoms. Bateman said the burden of the bird flu will reach far beyond birds, “This strain of the virus is a wide-ranging threat able to affect entire ecosystems. We must do what we can to protect them.” Banner image of a common dolphin (Delphinus delphis) spotted off a port in the state of Victoria. View five news elements
Time
Late August 2026 to September 2026.
Location and population
South Australia, including the Fleurieu Peninsula and Yorke Peninsula, with affected wildlife such as little penguins, magpies, crested terns, pelicans, red foxes, Australian sea lions, and common dolphins.
Event
H5N1 bird flu has been detected in marine mammals and wildlife in Australia, including a red fox in Adelaide and Australian sea lions and common dolphins in September 2026. The spread is attributed to infected wild bird species and is reported by state authorities and researchers.
Scale, impact and risk
H5N1 has been confirmed in multiple wildlife species, including critically endangered Australian sea lions. The virus can invade a mammal’s central nervous system, causing severe brain inflammation. Researchers warn of the potential for the virus to affect entire ecosystems.
Response, status and uncertainty
Authorities are monitoring the spread and have confirmed cases in marine mammals and wildlife. Efforts include removing carcasses to reduce viral reservoirs. The situation remains under investigation, with no definitive method to stop the spread yet.

H5N1 avian flu activity picking up in Minnesota as study notes virus in polar bears - CIDRAP

News Brief
The news report from CIDRAP dated September 24, 2026, highlights an increase in H5N1 avian flu activity in Minnesota. A study notes the presence of the virus in polar bears, suggesting potential environmental spread. However, the report does not provide specific case counts, death numbers, or detailed response measures. The focus is on the implications of the virus's presence in wildlife and its possible impact on ecosystems. The report emphasizes the need for continued monitoring and research but does not confirm any direct human health impact or official response actions. This information is part of a broader discussion on zoonotic disease outbreaks and their links to environmental and social factors.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

明尼苏达州H5N1禽流感活动增加,研究发现病毒在北极熊中存在

H5N1 avian flu activity picking up in Minnesota as study notes virus in polar bears
新闻简报文章报道了明尼苏达州H5N1禽流感活动的增加,美国农业部动植物卫生检验局(APHIS)在过去30天内记录了8例新的暴发。其中6例发生在明尼苏达州,包括两起影响51,500只鸟类的独立事件。最大的暴发发生在威斯康星州巴伦县,涉及79,100只鸟类。此外,一项发表在Science Direct上的研究发现,2023年和2024年阿拉斯加有三只死于的北极熊感染了高致病性H5 禽流感(HPAIV)2.3.4.4b亚型病毒。作者认为,尽管HPAIV可能对北极熊健康构成新兴威胁,但种群免疫力可能防止广泛的人口影响。文章还指出,在宾夕法尼亚州,美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)已确认一例麻疹死亡,但尚不清楚这指的是哪一病例。该州已报告四例近期麻疹死亡,CDC正在随着更多信息的出现更新其报告。文章强调宾夕法尼亚州的麻疹暴发仍在持续,这是美国规模最大的疫情,可能在规模上超过2025-2026年南卡罗来纳州的暴发。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发表于2026-09-24。明尼苏达州H5N1禽流感活动在过去10天内被注意到,过去30天内报告了暴发。阿拉斯加的北极熊研究涉及2023年和2024年的感染。
地点与对象
暴发发生在明尼苏达州,具体在巴伦县和斯蒂恩斯县,同时也发生在蒙大拿州和威斯康星州。北极熊研究是在阿拉斯加进行的。受影响的人群包括商业家禽、家庭养鸡场以及野生鸟类,如野鸭、加拿大雁和白头海雕。
事件
文章报道美国农业部动物检疫局在过去30天内记录了八例新的H5N1禽流感暴发,其中六例发生在明尼苏达州。一项新研究指出阿拉斯加北极熊中出现了H5N1禽流感。这些暴发包括商业和家禽 flock,以及 野生鸟类。
规模、影响与风险
在过去30天内,H5N1禽流感已确认在20个禽群中暴发,包括13个商业农场和7个家庭养鸡场。总共影响了560,000只鸟类。最大的暴发发生在威斯康星州巴伦县,影响了79,100只鸟类,而明尼苏达州的两起独立暴发则影响了51,500只鸟类。关于北极熊的研究表明,HPAIV感染可能代表对北极熊健康的新兴威胁。
应对、状态与不确定性
文章提到,美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)已确认宾夕法尼亚州一例麻疹死亡,但尚不清楚这指的是哪一病例。CDC正在随着更多信息的出现更新其报告。美国农业部APHIS正在监测暴发,而关于北极熊的研究仍在进行中,作者指出种群免疫力可能保护该物种免受广泛影响。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

H5N1 avian flu activity picking up in Minnesota as study notes virus in polar bears

News Brief
The article reports on the increasing activity of H5N1 avian flu in Minnesota, with the USDA Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service (APHIS) documenting eight new outbreaks in the past 30 days. Six of these outbreaks were in Minnesota, including two separate incidents affecting 51,500 birds. The largest outbreak was in Barron County, Wisconsin, involving 79,100 birds. Additionally, a study published in Science Direct found that three dead polar bears in Alaska were infected with highly pathogenic H5 avian influenza (HPAIV) clade 2.3.4.4b viruses in 2023 and 2024. The authors suggest that while HPAIV may pose an emerging threat to polar bear health, population immunity could prevent widespread demographic impacts. The article also notes that in Pennsylvania, the CDC has acknowledged one measles death, though it is unclear which case this refers to. The state has reported four recent measles deaths, and the CDC is updating its reporting as more information becomes available. The article highlights the ongoing measles outbreak in Pennsylvania, which is the largest in the country and could surpass the 2025-2026 South Carolina outbreak in scale.

Topics & Projects Antimicrobial Stewardship Chronic Wasting Disease Osterholm Update Podcasts Newsletters About CIDRAP CIDRAP in the News Our Staff Our Director Osterholm in the Press Shop Merchandise Contact Us About Search CLOSE Search Support Menu H5N1 avian flu activity picking up in Minnesota as study notes virus in polar bears News brief Stephanie Soucheray, MA Topics Avian Influenza (Bird Flu) Share Copied to clipboard rubyblossom/Flickr cc The US Department of Agriculture (USDA) Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service (APHIS) recorded eight new H5N1 avian flu outbreaks among commercial poultry, backyard flocks, and commercial turkey operations, with six of the outbreaks reported in Minnesota, while a new study reports H5 avian flu in polar bears in Alaska. Montana and Wisconsin each reported a single poultry outbreak. The largest outbreak was in Barron County, Wisconsin, with 79,100 affected birds at a commercial turkey meat operation. A total of 51,500 birds were sickened in two separate outbreaks at commercial turkey facilities in Stearns County, Minnesota. In the past 30 days H5N1 avian flu has been confirmed in 20 flocks, including 13 commercial farms and seven backyard flocks. A total of 560,000 birds have been affected. Minnesota also reported at least 40 wild-bird H5N1 avian flu detections in the past 10 days, most in the seven-county Minneapolos-St. Paul metro area. Mallards, Canada geese, and three bald eagles are among the wild birds. Avian flu in Alaskan polar bears In other news, a report this week in Science Direct notes that three dead polar bears in Alaska were infected with highly pathogenic H5 avian influenza (HPAIV) clade 2.3.4.4b viruses in 2023 and 2024. “These data indicate that HPAIV infection may represent an emerging threat to polar bear health within the Alaska Arctic; though, population immunity seems to be building and may protect this species from widespread demographic impacts of disease,” the authors said. Pennsylvania tops 800 measles cases as CDC acknowledges only 1 measles death News brief Stephanie Soucheray, MA Topics Measles Bilanol/iStock Measles cases in Pennsylvania have now reached 835, according to the state Department of Health yesterday, and 43 of them are new. In addition, the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) yesterday listed a single measles death for 2026 on its dashboard—its first confirmed death of 2026—but Pennsylvania is still reporting four recent deaths related to the virus. Unclear which death is being recognized It is unclear which death the CDC’s listing is referring to. An asterisk on the dashboard page says, “This number is subject to change; CDC will update its reporting as additional information becomes available and relevant reviews are completed.” Last week Pennsylvania officials warned the CDC they would not accept any agency aid in fighting the outbreak until the agency recognized the four deaths. Gov Josh Shapiro and Health and Human Services Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. got into a back-and-forth on X about determining if the case-patients had died “from” or “with” measles. The deaths were the first reported in Pennsylvania in 35 years. “The Secretary personally intervened in an otherwise administrative process to direct the CDC to not report these Pennsylvania deaths, presumably because it doesn’t fit his phony narrative,” Shapiro wrote on X last week. The state has the largest active outbreak in the country and could surpass the South Carolina outbreak (997 cases) of 2025-2026 as the largest outbreak in recent US history. CDC greenlights COVID vaccines for vulnerable children after nearly 1-month delay News brief Liz Szabo, MA Topics COVID-19 Childhood Vaccines mixetto/iStock After a nearly a month-long delay, the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) is now allowing states to order updated COVID-19 shots for a program that serves most US children. The Vaccines for Children (VFC) program offers free immunizations to more than half of US children, including those who are covered by Medicaid, the uninsured or underinsured, and Native Americans and Alaska Natives. Children whose families have private insurance have had access to the new COVID shots for weeks. The Food and Drug Administration approved the updated vaccines August 27, and companies began shipping doses to medical offices and pharmacies as early as September 27. Although overall hospitalization rates for COVID-19 are low, they have been rising since mid-June, and public health advocates say the delay in providing vaccines to the VFC program has left low-income children at higher risk. Along with adults over 65, hospitalization rates are highest for babies under 1 year old, according to the CDC. The news that vaccines for the VFC program are available was first reported by the Washington Post. States now able to order COVID vaccines Public health officials from multiple states confirmed that they are now able to order the free vaccines. South Carolina’s health department “was alerted yesterday afternoon by the CDC that COVID vaccines would be available for order today,” Casey White, a department spokeswoman, told CIDRAP News. “Announcements will be shared with VFC providers and health departments to communicate the opening of ordering for all respiratory immunizations.” 10 more sickened in Salmonella outbreak tied to broccoli sprouts News brief Chris Dall, MA Topics Foodborne Disease Salmonella CDC Ten more people have fallen ill in a multistate Salmonella outbreak linked to broccoli sprouts, the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) said in an update today. The outbreak of Salmonella Bovismorbificans has now sickened 32 people in six states, with three hospitalized. Illness-onset dates range from July 7 to September 3. Of the 27 people who’ve been interviewed, 26 reported eating sprouts or microgreens. Packaged broccoli sprouts grown by Evergreen Fresh Sprouts, LLC, and sold to grocery stores, restaurants, and foodservice customers in Idaho, Montana, and Washington have been recalled, the Food and Drug Administration said in its update on the investigation. Sprout samples collected by officials in Washington state tested positive for Salmonella Bovismorbificans, and whole-genome sequencing indicated the samples matched the outbreak strain. “If you have recalled broccoli sprouts in your home, throw them out or return them,” the CDC said. More Salmonella cases linked to pet turtles The CDC also recently provided an update on a Salmonella outbreak tied to pet turtles, which has now grown to 20 illnesses in 12 states—12 new cases and six newly affected states since July 27. Half of the illnesses are in children under age 5. The outbreak is caused by a strain of Salmonella Paratyphi B Variant L (+) Tartrate +. Of the 14 people who’ve been interviewed, 11 said they’d had contact with pet turtles before getting sick. The turtles were bought from pet stores, flea markets, and a reptile expo, the CDC said. H5N1在澳大利亚野生动物中传播,流感季加剧

H5N1 Spreads Among Australian Wildlife as Flu Season Ramps Up
新闻简报Australia has reported the spread of H5N1 avian influenza among wildlife, including seabirds, as flu season intensifies. The first confirmed mammal infection occurred on August 23, 2026, with a long-nosed fur seal testing positive. Subsequent detections included another fur seal near Kangaroo Island, red foxes, and common dolphins. As of September 17, 2026, 576 confirmed wildlife events have been reported, including mass mortality in greater crested terns. Australian authorities emphasize that there is no evidence of mammal-to-mammal transmission, and the risk to human health remains low. The World Health Organization notes no sustained person-to-person spread. Scientists are monitoring the virus for mutations that could enhance its adaptation to mammals and for signs of transmission between seals. The Global Virus Network (GVN) has called for continued surveillance, genomic analysis, and open data sharing to detect potential changes in the virus early. These efforts aim to better understand the risk posed by H5N1 as it circulates in wildlife.
查看新闻五要素
时间
澳大利亚野生动物中首次检测到H5N1是在2026年6月,截至2026年9月17日,后续检测陆续发生。首次确认的哺乳动物感染发生在2026年8月23日,另一只长鼻海豹在两天后被确认感染。
地点与对象
澳大利亚,特别是Kangaroo岛和南澳大利亚州,检测到多种哺乳动物物种,包括澳大利亚海豹、长鼻海豹、红狐和普通海豚。病毒还在多个地区被发现于野生鸟类中。
事件
澳大利亚当局于2026年8月23日确认了H5N1在哺乳动物中的首次感染,并在随后的其他哺乳动物物种中检测到感染。自2026年6月起,H5N1已在澳大利亚野生动物中传播,包括海鸟。这些感染正在被监测,以寻找病毒的潜在变化以及哺乳动物之间是否存在传播的证据。
规模、影响与风险
截至2026年9月17日,澳大利亚报告了576例确认的野生动物感染事件,包括大冠鸦鹃中的大规模感染。病毒已感染多种哺乳动物物种,但目前没有哺乳动物之间的传播证据。对人类健康的威胁被认为较低,尚未报告持续的人与人之间的传播。
应对、状态与不确定性
澳大利亚当局正在对病毒进行基因组分析,并监测可能增强其适应哺乳动物的特征。科学家们也在追踪这些感染是否代表来自鸟类的独立传播事件,或是海豹之间存在传播的证据。全球病毒网络(GVN)强调需要继续监测、基因组监测和开放共享研究结果,以便早期发现病毒的重要变化。

H5N1 Spreads Among Australian Wildlife as Flu Season Ramps Up

News Brief
Australia has reported the spread of H5N1 avian influenza among wildlife, including seabirds, as flu season intensifies. The first confirmed mammal infection occurred on August 23, 2026, with a long-nosed fur seal testing positive. Subsequent detections included another fur seal near Kangaroo Island, red foxes, and common dolphins. As of September 17, 2026, 576 confirmed wildlife events have been reported, including mass mortality in greater crested terns. Australian authorities emphasize that there is no evidence of mammal-to-mammal transmission, and the risk to human health remains low. The World Health Organization notes no sustained person-to-person spread. Scientists are monitoring the virus for mutations that could enhance its adaptation to mammals and for signs of transmission between seals. The Global Virus Network (GVN) has called for continued surveillance, genomic analysis, and open data sharing to detect potential changes in the virus early. These efforts aim to better understand the risk posed by H5N1 as it circulates in wildlife.
Original source text (English)
An influenza virus with surface glycoprotein spikes hemagglutinin (HA, trimer, red) and neuraminidase (NA, tetramer, purple). [KATERYNA KON/Science Photo Library/Getty Images] With flu season ramping up in certain areas of the world (it’s time to get that flu shot!) everyone is watching what strains are circulating—and how well they may match the current vaccine formulations. Some strains are watched more closely than others, and the highly pathogenic avian H5N1, a subtype of the influenza A virus that causes avian influenza (“bird flu”), may garner the most attention as it is regarded as one of the largest pandemic threats. Although H5N1 primarily infects birds, it can also infect mammals. Human infections are uncommon and have generally occurred after close contact with infected animals or contaminated environments. The World Health Organization reports no sustained person-to-person transmission. The Global Virus Network (GVN) is closely monitoring detections of H5N1 in several mammal species in Australia as the virus continues to spread. Last week, Australian authorities confirmed the first detection in an Australian sea lion, an endangered species, at Seal Bay on Kangaroo Island. Australia reported its first confirmed detection in a mammal on August 23, when a long-nosed fur seal found at Beachport in South Australia tested positive. A second long-nosed fur seal near Kangaroo Island was confirmed two days later and subsequent detections included red foxes and a common dolphin. Australian authorities report no evidence of mammal-to-mammal transmission of the virus in Australia. The detections show the virus infecting several mammal species in Australia within weeks of its first reported appearance in the country’s wildlife. Globally, H5N1 has infected a growing range of mammals, including seals and other marine mammals, dairy cattle, cats and wild carnivores. Scientists are monitoring these infections for changes in the virus and evidence of transmission between mammals. “Given what we have seen with this H5N1 genotype in seal and sea lion populations overseas, a spillover into Australian marine mammals was a possibility we had been concerned about and actively monitoring for,” said Michelle Wille, PhD, an avian influenza researcher affiliated with the University of Melbourne and the WHO Collaborating Centre for Reference and Research on Influenza and the Peter Doherty Institute for Infection and Immunity, a GVN Center of Excellence in Australia. “While it is sobering to see that possibility become a reality, the priority now is detailed genomic analysis of this and any future detections. We need to closely monitor for mutations associated with better adaptation to mammals and determine whether infections represent separate spillover events from birds or evidence of transmission between seals. That distinction will be critical to understanding the risk posed by the virus as it continues to circulate in wildlife.” Australia first detected H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b in wild seabirds in June. The virus has since been found in wild birds across several parts of the country. As of September 17, Australia had reported 576 confirmed events in wildlife, including mass mortality events in greater crested terns. An event may involve one or more animals. In April 2025, GVN published an analysis in The Lancet Regional Health—Americas calling for stronger surveillance, biosecurity, genomic monitoring and preparedness as H5N1 spread. In April 2026, GVN issued a statement on H5N1 vaccine developments, emphasizing the need for continued vaccine development and broader pandemic preparedness. “Australia is another reminder that H5N1 is a global animal health issue with implications for human health that we need to continue watching closely,” said Sten H. Vermund, MD, PhD, GVN’s chief medical officer and dean of the University of South Florida College of Public Health. “There is no reason for alarm, but there is every reason to remain vigilant. Surveillance across species, rapid genomic analysis and open sharing of what we learn give us the best chance to recognize an important change in the virus early.” News Birds Epidemiology Genome analysis Genotype Influenza Influenza vaccine Mammals
View five news elements
Time
The first detection in Australian wildlife was reported in June 2026, with subsequent detections as of September 17, 2026. The first confirmed mammal detection occurred on August 23, 2026, and a second long-nosed fur seal was confirmed two days later.
Location and population
Australia, specifically Kangaroo Island and South Australia, with detections in several mammal species including Australian sea lions, long-nosed fur seals, red foxes, and common dolphins. The virus has also been found in wild birds across multiple regions.
Event
Australian authorities confirmed the first detection of H5N1 in a mammal on August 23, 2026, and subsequent detections in other mammal species. The virus has been spreading among Australian wildlife, including seabirds, since June 2026. These infections are being monitored for potential changes in the virus and evidence of transmission between mammals.
Scale, impact and risk
As of September 17, 2026, Australia had reported 576 confirmed events in wildlife, including mass mortality events in greater crested terns. The virus has been found in multiple mammal species, but there is no evidence of mammal-to-mammal transmission. The risk to human health is considered low, with no sustained person-to-person transmission reported.
Response, status and uncertainty
Australian authorities are conducting genomic analysis of the virus and monitoring for mutations that could improve its adaptation to mammals. Scientists are also tracking whether infections represent separate spillover events from birds or evidence of transmission between seals. The Global Virus Network (GVN) has emphasized the need for continued surveillance, genomic monitoring, and open sharing of findings to detect important changes in the virus early.

Texas A&M AgriLife Tests Far-Ultraviolet Light to Prevent Bird Flu in Poultry - Quality Assurance & Food Safety

News Brief
Texas A&M AgriLife is testing far-ultraviolet light as a method to prevent bird flu in poultry, according to a report by Quality Assurance & Food Safety. The initiative is part of broader efforts in the food safety industry to combat avian influenza, which poses risks to both animal and human health. The report highlights the importance of innovative approaches in food safety, such as the use of advanced technologies to control pathogens. While the specific details of the testing process and its outcomes are not provided, the report indicates that this method is being explored as a potential solution to reduce the spread of bird flu in poultry facilities. The publication date of the report is September 24, 2026, and it is part of a series of articles on food safety and prevention strategies. The testing is currently under investigation, and further results are expected.

EFSA呼吁加强生物安全以应对禽流感风险挑战家禽供应韧性 - 食品原料第一

EFSA urges stronger biosecurity as bird flu risks challenge poultry supply resilience - Food Ingredients First
新闻简报欧洲食品安全局(EFSA)和欧洲委员会已呼吁加强生物安全措施,以应对由禽流感带来的风险,这些风险可能威胁家禽和鸡蛋供应链的稳定性。随着秋季迁徙季节的临近,这些机构强调了在农场层面进行预防的重要性,以避免暴发。此前的禽流感暴发已经扰乱了家禽生产,导致供应压力,并促使制造商关注供应商韧性以及应对动物源性原料潜在波动的策略。报告强调了禽流感对食品供应链的更广泛影响,特别是在受气候相关挑战影响的地区,如厄尔尼诺现象。尽管没有具体案例或死亡人数的报告,但对农业生产力和原料供应的威胁已被承认。目前的应对措施仍在进行中,现有证据中未显示有解决迹象。
查看新闻五要素
时间
这一举措旨在开发更具韧性的玉米杂交种,以提高对气候压力的耐受性,因为波动持续影响农业生产力和原材料供应。
地点与对象
欧洲、东南亚以及全球农业供应链。
事件
EFSA和欧洲委员会呼吁加强生物安全措施,以防止禽流感 暴发,这可能会扰乱家禽和鸡蛋供应链。该事件基于EFSA和欧洲委员会的官方声明确认。
规模、影响与风险
禽流感 暴发此前已扰乱家禽生产,并导致供应压力。影响规模包括对家禽和鸡蛋衍生原料的可用性和稳定性可能造成潜在干扰,现有证据中未报告具体案例或死亡人数。
应对、状态与不确定性
EFSA和欧洲委员会正在关注农场层面的预防措施,以避免暴发。目前的应对措施仍在进行中,现有证据中未显示有解决迹象。内容状态为完整,来源为权威媒体。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

EFSA urges stronger biosecurity as bird flu risks challenge poultry supply resilience - Food Ingredients First

News Brief
The European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) and the European Commission have issued a call for stronger biosecurity measures to address the risks posed by avian influenza, which could threaten the stability of poultry and egg supply chains. As the autumn migration season approaches, these entities are emphasizing the importance of farm-level prevention to avoid outbreaks. Previous bird flu outbreaks have already disrupted poultry production, leading to supply pressures and prompting manufacturers to focus on supplier resilience and strategies to manage potential volatility in animal-derived ingredients. The report highlights the broader implications of avian influenza on food supply chains, particularly in regions affected by climate-related challenges such as El Niño. While no specific case or death counts are reported, the risk to agricultural productivity and ingredient availability is acknowledged. The response remains ongoing, with no indication of resolution in the current evidence.

Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza Detected in a Multi-Species Backyard Flock in Boone County - foodmarket.com

News Brief
A news report from foodmarket.com, published on September 23, 2026, confirmed the detection of highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) in a multi-species backyard flock in Boone County. The report highlights the presence of HPAI in the area, which poses a localized risk to the flock and potentially to other animals. However, no specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers were provided. The report does not detail the response measures taken, but it notes that the Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service (APHS) in Texas has released a new world screwworm infested zone, which is unrelated to the HPAI event. The information is based on a full original report body, and the event is classified as confirmed. The report does not mention any further details about the scale of the outbreak or the actions being taken to address it.
